Yes, we want to further develop the EU in accordance with the principle of subsidiarity, which was already very important to the founding fathers of the European Union. That is why it is good – and I am pleased about it – that German Minister Gernot Blümel is today challenging the first hour of Europe in this legislative period, because he will have a central task in the autumn when we bring this presidency to the stage. Where is the European Union at the beginning of 2018? What will be on the agenda if we hold the Presidency? – Economically, and we have heard this before in the debate, the EU – and with that the public budgets of the Member States – are actually very positive. We have a situation that we haven't had in the last ten years: The overall debt ratio is declining, unemployment is falling, we have a record employment rate. So you could think everything was fine. - No, it's not that way! Of course, we also have difficult open questions, which in the second half of the year will sometimes even culminate when I think of the Brexit which we have to conclude in the negotiations. Our Presidency, and it is the third for Austria – we already had the EU Presidency in 1998 and 2006 – will be in a new form this time. There is now a so-called trio presidency. This trio presidency will be formed with Estonia, which held the presidency in the previous half of the year, with Bulgaria, which it held in the first half of 2018, and with Austria, and especially the Estonians – a small country – have shown that in Europe not only the Great – the Germans, the French – have the saying, but also that a small country can not only be noticed, but can make progress very decisive. In digitalization, the Estonians are also a model for us, and we will continue their work accordingly during our presidency, but – I have already said this – new tasks such as Brexit will be added. Yes, it was certainly a serious loss for the European Union when the British decided on their referendum in June 2016, for it is not only the loss of more than 10 billion euros of this second largest net payer of the European Union, but it is also for me the loss of many of the contributions made by the British that will be missing from this European Union. Especially in transatlantic relations, Britain was very important for the European Union, for example, as a permanent member of the Security Council, and there are many other areas in which we are going to miss the British. It is now about bringing this Brexit to the best possible end and having the British, the United Kingdom, as a good neighbor in the future. This is also very important for Austria and will be one of the key tasks of our Presidency. The multi-annual financial framework is directly linked to it, because if there is less money, then the more careful it is to deal with the money. This will be our guiding principle when we enter the negotiations with regard to the next multiannual financial framework, because after Great Britain's withdrawal, there will be less money in the European Union, and the more careful we have to deal with this money. A third point that I am allowed to address is very important, and there is a Europe-wide movement in the direction that German Chancellor Sebastian Kurzal has always called for to advance the protection of the external borders of the European Union, as we then distribute refugees. It is quite, very important that Germany has also come into motion. The last statements made by the German Minister of the Interior are very positive to me that this concept, which our Federal Chancellor has represented, is now seen in this way across Europe, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] That we move migration and security issues to the centre of our presidency is what the Austrian population rightly expects from us. I should like to mention another fourth point: Austria has always been very important that the Western Balkans have an accession perspective. As a foreign minister, Alois Mock was a pioneer, for example, in Croatia, as far as Slovenia is concerned, which is now valuable members of the European Union. This prospect of accession also needs other Western Balkans. That is why it is a good thing for Bulgaria to see this during the current trio presidency, as we see it, and then, after us, two states will again be part of the trio presidency, which will continue this focus, which will be the Romanians and the Croatians who hold the Presidency. First: Since the Treaty of Lisbon, as a national parliament, we have been granted a range of cooperation opportunities that we did not have before. Yes, we are also part of parliamentary control at the European level. Of course, our members of the government, who are represented in the Council, have spoken and answered here. We have also said that we want the Members of the European Parliament to speak here too; I believe that today our Head of Delegation, Othmar Karas, will also speak here as one of the Members of the European Parliament. This interplay is very important. If the European Commission makes proposals which are not our own taste, we have the opportunity, together with the other European Union parliaments, to show the Commission the yellow card: That's not it! We have the possibility of these subsidiarity complaints; and especially the Federal Council, our second Chamber, is very active in this area. If, however, a legislative decision is taken at European level which we are not satisfied with, we as the National Council can also address a subsidiarity action to the Court of Justice of the European Union. But before it comes to that, it is always important that we vote well with the other parliaments. Six years before Austria joined the European Union, which was already in 1989, the European Committees of National Parliaments met in Cosac to play an active part in the development of Europe on the part of national parliaments from our national parliaments. We will also have two important Cosac conferences in Vienna in the second half of the year, in the context of which we will have subsidiarity, namely: What is to be done at European level, where do we need more Europe? – there are also these areas – but also: What should be better regulated at national level? I would like to address a very important point, namely the common security and defence policy. We have also had a joint conference on this subject since 2012, at which the relevant committee chairmen will meet in Europe. I gather, my dear. Ladies and gentlemen: That is why not only the Federal Government will benefit from this opportunity of the EU Presidency, but also we as a whole of Parliament are invited to take action together in accordance with state policy, so that this presidency will become one that the Republic of Austria can be very satisfied with itself. We said as a coalition, we want to choose the scenario 4: ‘Less but more efficient’. This means that the European Union should be given more competence in areas where it can do things better than the national states, but that we want to deal with those areas where the national states can do better, here in Parliament and in their own federal government. We from the European Parliament are partners, we are companions; and we parliamentarians should make every effort to make the EU more democratic that it will not become an EU of governments and transparency, but that citizens and parliaments will determine the future of the European Union. ParlaMint-AT_2018-01-31-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00007_d7e9072	High house! Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to thank you for the opportunity to be able to make a statement here in the House on the implementation of the government programme and on the subject of education, science and research. The improvement of the framework conditions for schools, universities and universities as well as for non-university research institutions is for me a central and ultimately also the immanent task of a specialist minister in this field. I know that we live in a poor country and that education, science and research are the engines of social and economic development. I am also convinced that for individuals, the acquisition of education involves an intellectual emancipation process capable of critically assessing itself, but also the social environment. Education is an important and historically deep gain of freedom for each individual in an enlightened society and is therefore so immensely important, and education is also adopted and is becoming more and more important in terms of quantity. At present, almost 40 percent of a year of birth completes the school with Matura, two thirds of which then go to a university or college. The other half of a year of birth is a dual training, which is an essential part of our education system. Austria is today one of the richest countries in the EU or the world, perhaps particularly because of human capital or, simply, because of the good training opportunities available in Austria, and the ability of its inhabitants. In my opinion, our education system is in principle well established. I'm not a friend of alarmism. We have created good conditions to support the development towards education society and knowledge economy. There are detailed measures to make it even more powerful, but in principle our education system is well established. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] A measure I have started with my house, with the Ministry, is the introduction of German funding classes. I have already encouraged this concept of the German funding classes in my former secondary job as Chairman of the Expert Council for Integration in 2014. In Austria, children with non-German first language often have more difficulty than the average of the children with German as the first language, in school competence, as well as in the finals. This can also be seen in measurements of reading skills at the end of primary schooling. In doing so, the performance differences between those children with and those without migration backgrounds of more than 50 points or nearly two years of learning are very significant. At Pisa 2015, these differentiations also resulted in: Austria is one of the countries with the greatest power difference between children with and without migration backgrounds. These large performance differences do not disappear even if one introduces the social status as a control variable. Therefore, before entering the regular school, students should have enough German skills – and this is a measure that is planned – to be able to follow the instruction immediately. Side and cross-country beginners, but also children who come from the kindergarten are often classified as extraordinary students because their German skills are not sufficient. I have developed the model implemented by my predecessor. A key feature of this is the creation of classes of German funding for those children who, because of their lack of competence in the teaching language, cannot follow the teaching. Whether they can follow the instruction or not, a standardized language test will be used to ensure a correct diagnosis of the language level. German funding classes get curriculum, which is a form of intensification. The German funding classes have an hourly length of 15 hours in basic and 20 hours in secondary level I. The remaining hours are spent together with the other children, in those subjects that may be less linguistic, such as gymnastics, drawing and music. I want there to be some sharing with the children in a regular school class. The theoretically worked is also to be tested in concrete communication with the same age. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] The German funding classes are organized in a number of ways. If the knowledge has been improved accordingly and students can follow normal education, they should switch to it as soon as possible – but also as competently as necessary. If we transfer and transfer students from these German funding classes to the regular classes, we do not leave them alone: The German funding courses are intended to join it. This means a gradual withdrawal of the funding measure and a gradual extension of participation in regular education. In this context, I should like to make an announcement: If it succeeds in implementing a second compulsory kindergarten year, especially for those who need special German support, then we will gain a lot in this area. Today, we see at the level of three to six years old, that a total of 30 percent needs a language support. And even when the children have completed the kindergarten, many who do not have enough German skills to follow the lessons immediately remain. They are then classified as extraordinary students. I am concerned that all children who are trained and trained in a second compulsory kindergarten year can start the folk school without any start-up disadvantages. Overall, it is also my concern that the kindergarten is understood as a first educational institution, just like the École matenelle in France. This certainly requires more than just the academicisation of the training of kindergarten teachers. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] In these first weeks in which I was allowed to work as a minister, we have also put in place further measures: We have looked at newsletters and releases and have seen to what extent they are necessary. We have also shifted the forced entry into the new upper level a little in order to ultimately gain time for an evaluation. Of course, we are also building up the ministry, which consists of two ministries, accordingly. All of this is important, but I refer here to the topic “The future of Austria through education and science”, and I would like to remain a little more meaningful. To secure the future of Austria also means to strengthen universities in a sustainable way. This was the aim of an amendment from June 2017, with which the universities were given more money, and it is also the The aim of today's Council of Ministers' decision and the secondary legislative proposal on university financing Newly, with which a new financing regime can be started in universities. With this concept, university funding takes place from the traditional – we do it as we always did – and each university gets as much as it was during the last funding period – and goes quite clearly towards the funding of the tasks and achievements that a university performs. In the future, the university budget will be calculated from the sum of the actively cared for students, weighted by subject groups, and from the number of researchers, weighted by subject groups, plus other competitive components. If a university is very successful in recruiting third-party funds, then it will get more, and if a university is very friendly – student-related is very friendly – and brings many students to graduation, it will also be rewarded under the title of this competitive component. [[Congratulations with ÖVP and FPÖ.]] I would also like to say here that university funding has two clear objectives: On the one hand, the aim is to improve research at universities and thus infrastructure, equipment and personnel, and on the other hand to improve the conditions for study. The long study periods, the high drop-out – even if they are not always a real drop-out, I know – but also the relatively low graduation rates are serious criticism points in the university system. With 500 jobs – however, depending on the decision of the autonomous university, there can be more if you not only rely on professorships, but perhaps also on senior lecturers or other job profiles; there will in all cases be more staff at the universities – you can change something about the studyability of individual curriculum. However, I must also say that planning places are a prerequisite for improved study conditions. The universities should be able to exercise their due diligence towards students, and to do so they need instruments to bring the number of persons taken into line with the capacities. A certain number of professors can only care for a certain number of students responsibly. If there is a sudden run on a specific curriculum at a university, the university may also ask the relevant ministers for help to say how fair access management methods can produce a realistic number. Admission procedures are provided for in the law, but those that also include something like fitness feedback – a very reasonable idea in itself –: Students should recognize for themselves whether they are suitable for this or that study before admission. Such an aptitude check without further binding force for the admission procedure relies on the reflexive power of the respective students. I am therefore pleased that the rectors, as representatives of the universities as a whole, are in principle positive towards our proposals and that the new financing system – I quote – is a great, good and enjoyable step in European normality. [[Consistency with ÖVP and FPÖ as well as the Abg. Stroll.]] My last point in connection with research and the future: I announce the preparation and development of a post-2020 research strategy. This is a for- This strategy is based on an OECD evaluation procedure, which is currently critical of what our research promotion and funding system looks like. In this context, I would once again like to make such a point as a pact for research and development in connection with a research funding law. You may have discussed this more often, we at the universities as well, but it has not been realized so far. We need planning security in the field of research promotion, because it makes little sense to build highly specialized research institutions, but perhaps after three years to say, sorry, it doesn't matter with the money. We want to achieve a certain degree of planning security in the field of research, also with an ambitious research funding rate of 3.76 percent, which is provided for in the Government Agreement. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the FPÖ.]] High house! As far as my statement and my report on the current and planned measures are concerned. Education, science and research are central areas of the future security of Austria. I also ask for your support in the area, as far as this is politically possible. How does the present draft of the first Austrian universal rental law intend to reach fair prices for the living room in a modern way? – First of all, through transparency: The law is understandable, even when it comes to price formation. It is clear to everyone that you can calculate the rental height yourself and that it also holds in court. The question now arises, of course, who is interested in the fact that this is not transparently regulated in the law. For example, these are the landlords. This is also the special feature of this new system to remove these speculation supplements and to reward actual investments. The ecological standard also plays a very important role: Our rental right would be Europe's most ecological rental right. In summary, this means: durable rents for contemporary apartments, generous surcharges for investments from the landlord side, and also ecological construction should pay off. The third point is fair and transparent operating costs, which, of course, also concern existing contracts. That is to say, the catalogue of operating costs belongs through researched, it belongs to an accurate billing, in which the credits and repayments are also billed on a day-to-day basis. I was a long tenant's representative, and I can tell you that, even if the tenants have enforced and won the challenge, the landlords have very often neglected with the other tenants in the house or even with the post-chambers these illegal supplements and discounts have continued to demand. Consequently, criminal provisions must also be laid down for such practices. This co-existence between landlords and tenants is important to us. Our long-term project – the creation of a modern and modern rental law – is also about a fair balance of interests between landlords and tenants, market-compliant rents in new buildings and total renovations of buildings to a modern standard. In the future, renting interest will no longer be important, as is the old building in which the apartment is located, but the quality of the apartment. This federal government will therefore maintain the co-operation and not the one against the other. We understand that by the new style. Thank you very much.

In two cases, the procedure was adjusted in accordance with § 190 Z 1 and § 190 Z 1 respectively. § 190 Z 2 StPO proceeds because in the accused the proof of the intention to re-act in the National Socialist sense was not possible and in the face of individual acts already the objective action was not realized. In one case, in view of the fact that proceedings had already been initiated for comparable actions, the absence of evidence on the basis of re-activities was excluded from the initiation of an investigation procedure.’ On your questions relating to the subject of legal extremism: I have to hold on in principle – and that is why I made this reference to the existing legal situation at the beginning of my remarks – and right-wing extremism is not per se prohibited under Austrian law, and for this reason, right-wing extremism cannot be prosecuted under criminal law. What is traceable and punishable is the re-activating of the National Socialist Party, and a statistical collection or classification of persons or organizations as a far right does not take place either, which is logical. A statistics on acts of action, as well as on ads and offences in the context of extremism is published in the annual Constitutional Protection Report. An evaluation by indicated persons is not possible alone because many ads are directed against unknown perpetrators, whose number is simply and grippingly unknown. If you now want to know figures from the relevant constitutional protection reports relating to advertising in the field of right-wing extremism, I can tell you: In 2013 there were 1 186, in 2014 1 201, in 2015 1 691, in 2016 1 867. The NS Prohibition Act in 2013 included 529 ads, in 2014 663, in 2015 953 and in 2016 884. The figures for the 2017 Constitutional Protection Report will be published at the end of April, beginning of May 2018. And now on your question as to right-wing extremism, whose fight we supposedly refuse. I have already told you that the fight against right-wing extremism is a central concern and is taken seriously by us. But something else is the matter of appearing in the form of a separate report. We have decided to consider the danger of extremism in general and as a holistic threat and to publish it accordingly. This means that the Constitutional Protection Report will continue to include all aspects of extremism, that is, right-wing extremism, that is, left-wing extremism and that is religiously motivated extremism.

It is therefore particularly important to me to pay attention to the fact that the laws we decide to bring relief to people in reality. On this subject, an example from my district: It does not make it easier for you to live if you have rented an apartment that is 68 square meters tall in this example and for which a rental price of 545 euros is agreed without operating costs, and then you will find interesting supplements on the invoice when checking by an expert. A heater in the bathroom is therefore worth a supplement of 2 percent, and did you know that renovated plastic windows can make up a supplement of 7.5 percent? When all the rooms are walkable from the foreroom, this costs another 5 percent surcharge. Connections for washing machines, empty pipes for the telephone or even the maintenance status of the street facade are also possibilities for surcharges that have no legal basis! These are interesting examples of how in practice the rental price is pushed up at any rate. For the apartment addressed, the rent had to be reduced by EUR 150 per month on the basis of the review by an expert. With the model of universal rental law proposed by us, the same apartment would cost 306 euros with a single purchasing system – that would be another 90 euros left to the tenant. In addition, he should not be concerned about the review of his rental price by experts, courts and lawyers. In this sense, it brings security to citizens when they know how their rental price is composed, when they know that there is a legal solution for surcharges and discounts that guarantee a fair rental price. My appeal therefore: Support You're our model, because it's fair, transparent and just!

[[President Bures shall take over the chairmanship.]] However, the abolition of cold progression also has a distribution policy effect, which should not be ignored. What is this distribution policy effect? . It is that if the abolition of cold progression exceeds the average rate of inflation, as provided for in your proposal, this will result in the higher incomes being overcompensated and the low incomes below the lower incomes will be compensated. Why is that so? . I declare it to you, Mr Strolz: The average inflation rate for lower incomes is different from that for higher incomes. If so, then! So why not adapt all the benefits on the expenditure side to the inflation rate? Then: That's where people lose year-to-year. For a model of the abolition of cold progression over the average inflation rate, I am not, honestly, to have. What I am missing from your proposal, Mr Strolz – you have spoken about sustainability – is the following: You also need to explain to me how you want to finance the abolition of cold progression. However, it has also been crystallized that, as the Republic of Austria, we have had problems at all corners and ends in all parts of the process, from the first call for proposals for this fighter aircraft system and the failure of this first call for tender, so that we have needed a second one, to the concrete assessment of the offers that are available, to the negotiations that have been concluded then, and after the conclusion of the contract, the failure to comply with the Treaty which we have concluded. Now, quite specifically – I want to make this a little stronger here, too, because I think that's important –: We have seen that lobbyists from the arms industry – and that could be any other industry in the future – have virtually been flawed through our ministries like a knife through warm butter. We have seen that in the Ministry of Economic Affairs, two persons who were responsible for the counter-business had to be deducted. We have seen that, for example, a former Minister of Defense has exceeded its powers in comparative negotiations and has concluded contracts for the significant damage of the Republic. We have seen that, according to the documents available to us, there have been numerous flows of money, which were at least unexplainable. Vector Aerospace, who probably managed Airbus in black cash registers by then EADS, has made a variety of payments to people in Austria and abroad, with either not being clear about what the return was, or the return was not documented accordingly, or has shown that the recorded counter-performance was actually based on counterfeiting. I should like to remind my colleagues from the Committee of Inquiry that we have individual best interests. In the case of the same person, they had signatures and signatures, but they were shown to have been made by three different persons. That is to say, we are talking about a situation in which the Republic of Austria failed at all levels in a large acquisition, then the largest acquisition. Now one could think that the important two of the four questions were answered that it had been studied enough on the subject of Eurofighter. This is not correct because we have not yet learned our lessons and because some questions are still very central. The points that have not yet been answered focus mainly on the question: What did the Republic not communicate upon conclusion of a contract? What should have been communicated? Or also: What did the government of that time not communicate to Parliament to the investigative committee or to the actors, the legislator? I say quite clearly, I am not talking about a counter-play of red and black-blue or anything else. All the players we heard at the time are no longer players in politics. It's not about party color, it's about reconnaissance, so we can get answers and learn from mistakes. The danger of the whole thing being turned away is more up-to-date than ever. We know that, for example, several public prosecutors are currently in charge of investigations, in Munich, in Rome, also in Vienna. We know that the Munich Public Prosecutor's Office – which seems to be possible there – was offered a comparison payment of 90 million euros from Airbus when the investigations were stopped. We know from media reports that the new government or the new Minister of Defence has apparently been offered a so-called Susi-Sorglos package. I can't answer why it's called Susi Sorglos, but the value here is a retrofitting of the fighter planes. There has also been an amount of up to 80 million euros, possibly even 100 million euros. But now I ask myself the following question: If a group is willing to take so much money into its hands, of course there are still two possible responses. The first is: You want to avert an image damage. That would be justified because a group would like to add a new chapter. But the second is: He wants to distract from an investigation. I believe that in the last committee of inquiry we have shown that we have been very respectful between the political groups, but also with those who have not been consulted by short-term stays abroad. So we are talking about making decisions in these weeks and months to see if further investigations are to be made or not. And we are also talking about the need to ask ourselves as a Parliament – and, of course, the government itself – how we deal with the findings from committee of inquiry. One is that we want a new establishment so that the clarification of the last two open questions can also take place. The other is, however, that we want a seriousity after committee of inquiry, so that the findings of the legislator are actually implemented. I would just like to draw up a few examples of what we have taken as a learner for our group at the Eurofighter Investigation Committee. I think that too many of these findings can be found in some political groups. The first was that we said, we must have the Strengthen economic and corruption prosecutorship because we have seen that the resources available there are not enough for the investigation of such major scandals. We've seen this at Hypo Alpe-Adria, we've seen it at the Eurofighter. There are too few resources to be able to explain accordingly. The second was that we wanted freedom of guidance for the prosecutors, so that the freedom and independence of the prosecutors is indeed guaranteed. The third was that we wanted to debate at least the introduction of minimum penalties and the raising of maximum penalties for bribery and bribery, whether this is a place in our legal system. We wanted a public hearing of candidates for ministerial offices, because we saw that the Minister of Defence at that time obviously could not exercise his office to the extent that one could have expected – and we believe that this would have already occurred in a ministerial hearing. We wanted a cooling-off phase for former ministers, because it can't be that former ministers change sides and then cash hundreds of thousands of euros on commissions. We also wanted to change counter-businesses, because, while the Republic is setting up billions of counter-businesses in a contract, it then puts three people who are not trained to do so in a ministry that falls within the range of good lobbyism or even bad. All of this we already knew after the last committee of inquiry, the one to the Hypo Alpe-Adria has gained much more knowledge. So far, nothing has been implemented. I would ask you, ladies and gentlemen, that you support our request in the committee responsible, that you enable us to carry out the information work, but also that, after the information has been informed, you will have the consequences, because – and I am convinced of this –: Transparency is reasonable to citizens in this country. - Thank you.
